*Sticheron 3:* The assembly of the venerable, having set forth, easily trampled bodily passions underfoot to the end, receiving through the practice of virtues the mind as sovereign; whereby they valiantly conquered all demonic wiles, and appeared as companions of the angels, having lived as bodiless ones. With them they now rejoice together, dwelling brightly in the mansions on high, and they stand before Christ, asking from Him that great mercy be granted unto our souls.[^6317]

*Other Stichera to the Martyrs, same Tone:*[^6318]

*Sticheron 4:* Clothed with the shield of faith, and strengthening themselves with the sign of the Cross, Thy holy ones, O Lord, bravely gave themselves over unto torments, and abolished the pride and deceit of the devil. Through their prayers, as Thou art almighty God, send down peace unto the world, and great mercy unto our souls.[^6319]

*Sticheron 5:* Caring nothing for all earthly things, and bravely venturing into torments, ye failed not of blessed hopes; but ye became heirs of the heavenly kingdom, O all-praised martyrs. Having boldness toward God, the Lover of mankind, ask peace for the world, and great mercy for our souls.[^6320]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:*[^6321]
In the Red Sea an image of the unwedded Bride was inscribed of old: there Moses, the divider of the water; here Gabriel, the minister of the miracle. Then Israel traversed the deep dryshod; now the Virgin hath given birth unto Christ without seed. The sea remained impassable after the passage of Israel; the Immaculate One remained incorrupt after the birth of Emmanuel. O Thou Who art and Who wast before, and hast appeared as man, O God, have mercy on us.[^6322]

*O Gladsome Light: Prokeimenon of the day. Vouchsafe, O Lord, on this evening:*[^6323]

*On the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 5:*[^6324]

*Sticheron 1:* Pray for us, O holy martyrs, that we may be delivered from our iniquities; for unto you grace hath been given to pray for us.[^6325]

*Verse: Many are the afflictions of the righteous, and the Lord will deliver them out of them all.*[^6326]

*Sticheron 2:* With insatiable love of soul, ye denied not Christ, O holy martyrs: ye endured cruel and diverse wounds of sufferings, ye cast down the pride of the tormentors, and having kept the faith unbending and unharmed, ye were translated unto the heavens. Therefore, having boldness toward Him, ask that great mercy be granted unto us.[^6327]

*Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):*[^6328]
*Verse: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken to Thyself, O Lord.*[^6329]
*Sticheron 3:* I remembered the Prophet crying: I am earth and ashes; and again I looked into the tombs, and I beheld the naked bones, and I said: Who then is a king, or a warrior? A rich man, or a pauper? A righteous man, or a sinner? But give rest, O Lord, with the righteous unto Thy servants, as Thou art the Lover of mankind.[^6330]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:*[^6331]
O thou full of grace, intercede by thy prayers, and ask for our souls a multitude of compassions, and the cleansing of many transgressions, we pray thee.[^6332]

*Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart: Trisagion; after Our Father: troparion, and dismissal.*[^6333]

*On Friday at Small Compline,*
*Supplicatory Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos.*
*Tone 5. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^6334]
Horse and rider into the Red Sea Christ shook off, shattering battles with an high arm; and He saved Israel, who sang a song of victory.[^6335]

*Refrain: Most Holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*[^6336]

*Troparion 1:* All human generations praise thee, as of old, O Virgin, thou didst prophetically foretell; accept me also who hymn thee, O Lady, and enlighten me and give me understanding.[^6337]

*Troparion 2:* Thou hast blunted the sting of death and the sin of the world, O Virgin, having given birth unto true Life; for whose sake, O Lady, blunt speedily the sharp arrows of my passions.[^6338]

*Glory:* Thou alone, having appeared from of old adorned with virginal fringes, didst tear asunder the fig garment of Adam; therefore clothe me now with the garment of chastity, by thy prayers.[^6339]

*Both now:* Many daughters indeed acquired wealth and divine glory before, manifestly; but beyond comparison, O Lady, thou hast surpassed them all. Therefore enrich me now with heavenly and divine graces.[^6340]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* O Thou Who didst establish the earth upon nothing by Thy command, and didst suspend it while it hung unsupported by weight: upon the immovable rock of Thy commandments, O Christ, establish Thy Church, O only Good One, and Lover of mankind.[^6341]

*Troparion 1:* The depth and height were recognized in thee, O all-pure Virgin,[^6342] *Troparion 1 (continued):* of the unsearchable Wisdom of God, who wast born of thy womb, O Virgin; through which depth deliver my heart from the serpent's counsels.[^6343]

*Troparion 2:* Thou Who of old didst form from the water by Thy word the winged fowl and creeping things, which were not before: Thou straightway, O Christ, from the blood of the pure Virgin hast fashioned a strange garment of Thy divine incarnation.[^6344]

*Glory:* Thou alone hast appeared as a cleansing unto nature, O All-Immaculate One, in whom the divine Fire dwelt unburnt, plainly cleansing it: do thou therefore cleanse the defilement of the passions of my transgressions, and by thy prayers enlighten me.[^6345]

*Both now:* Knowing thee, O Pure One, to be the cup of the new vineyard's branches that manifestly refreshes the faithful unto the remission of transgressions, I pray thee: by the outpouring of divine water refresh my heart.[^6346]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* Perceiving Thy divine exhaustion, Habakkuk cried out with trembling unto Thee, O Christ, with foresight: For the salvation of Thy people, to save Thine anointed ones, art Thou come.[^6347]

*Troparion 1:* Thou hast appeared as an unploughed furrow, which hast budded forth the unseeded divine ear of grain; whereby do thou feed me who hunger, both with divine gifts and graces.[^6348]

*Troparion 2:* When I am truly sick unto death, and held fast by the fire of soul-destroying passions, O Lady, having watered me with the water of thy prayers, raise me up speedily.[^6349]

*Glory:* As being the ensouled city of God, whose spiritual rivers make glad its streams: by the pillar of thy prayers establish the dwelling of my soul.[^6350]

*Both now:* Knowing thee to be the cloud that rained down true righteousness, I pray thee, O all-pure Lady: deliver thy servant speedily from all who wrong me.[^6351]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* Clothed with light as with a garment, rising early unto Thee I call: Enlighten my darkened soul, O Christ, as Thou art alone compassionate.[^6352]

*Troparion 1:* The disturbance and surge of sin and of my passions, O Virgin Lady, calm completely, who hast given birth unto the Cause of dispassion.[^6353]

*Troparion 2:* Thou hast appeared as the loom from on high of Christ, of the beauty of divine raiment; with the garment of virtues, O Pure One, clothe my naked soul.[^6354]

*Glory:* Grant me cleansing of transgressions by thy supplications, O pure Virgin, who didst bear for us the divine Cleansing, Christ and the Lord.[^6355]

*Both now:* Wounds, sores, and sinful festerings, by the handiwork of prayers consume for me, O Virgin, and grant strength unto thy servant.[^6356]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* Christ the Master, raging with a soul-destroying tempest, calm the sea of passions, and lead me up from corruption, as Thou art compassionate.[^6357]

*Troparion 1:* Having given birth unto the light that made the luminaries, enlighten my soul now, and deliver me from the darkening of passions, O All-Radiant One.[^6358]

*Troparion 2:* Entreat thy Son, O Birthgiver of God, Who of old sweetened the waters of Marah, to deliver me speedily from grievous passion and bitterness.[^6359]

*Glory:* The torrent of passions disturbs my soul, O All-Immaculate One: but dry it up by thy prayers, and destroy evil thoughts.[^6360]

*Both now:* Christ, redeeming mankind, came forth from Zion, from thy womb, O all-immaculate Lady; whereby also deliver me from temptations and afflictions.[^6361]

*Then, Lord, have mercy (three times). Glory... both now...*[^6362]

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 5:* Why, being despondent, O passionate soul, hast thou forgotten thy God Who hath mercy upon thee? And despising His commandments, dost thou foolishly finish thy life in fornication? Depart from malice, crying unto the Birthgiver of God: Have mercy upon my despondent soul.[^6363]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* The supremely exalted Lord of the fathers quenched the flame and bedewed the youths, who sang in unison: Blessed art Thou, O God![^6364]

*Troparion 1:* The demonic hosts are terrified and tremble at thy invocation, O All-Immaculate One; delivering me from them, save and preserve me, sheltering me from every harm.[^6365]

*Troparion 2:* Ineffable is thy glory, O Virgin, for thou didst bear the Lord of glory; therefore by thy prayers make me worthy of the glory of thy Son and my God.[^6366]

*Glory:* Incline Thyself unto the supplications of thy servant, O Lady, and deliver me speedily from perils and afflictions, and from all temptations, visible and invisible.[^6367]

*Both now:* Wash me clean with purity, who am wholly defiled and wallowed in passions of shame, O All-Immaculate One, and make me bright, O Virgin, by the prayers of thy sprinklings.[^6368]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* Unto Thee, the Creator of all, the children in the furnace, having woven a world-wide choir, sang: All ye works, praise the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages.[^6369]

*Troparion 1:* The passionate water of barrenness made barren and dried up the womb of my soul; rain down upon me, O light cloud, divine dew, that I may bring forth fruits of repentance.[^6370]

*Troparion 2:* Calm the surge of passionate thoughts and the storm, O All-Immaculate One, by thy prayers; and guide me unto the stream of dispassion, that I may fervently glorify thee unto all ages.[^6371]

*Glory:* O spiritual door, and closed gate through which God alone passed: bar and close the doors of my passions, and open unto me the gates of hope.[^6372]

*Both now:* Take from me the burden of transgressions, O Virgin Mother: who ineffably didst bear the Lamb of God and the Word, Who alone takes away the whole sin of the entire world.[^6373]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* Rejoice, O Isaiah! The Virgin had in her womb, and brought forth a Son, Emmanuel, both God and man, the Orient is His name; Whom magnifying, we call the Virgin blessed.[^6374]

*Troparion 1:* Having defiled my soul with many transgressions as a man, and being mired in carnal passions, I now pray unto thee diligently and entreat thee: Cleanse me, O Pure One, by thy prayers, from every malice.[^6375]

*Troparion 2:* Thou hast borne the Wisher of mercy, the God of compassions and Lover of mankind, the only Good, Long-suffering, and Compassionate One: show Him to be propitious unto me, O Pure One, by thy supplications, and grant forgiveness of transgressions.[^6376]

*Glory:* Being held fast by the slumber of despondency, and now sleeping in pleasures, O Lady, raise up thy servant unto the virtues of good deeds, by thy vigilant maternal prayers.[^6377]

*Both now:* Blessing I bless thee, O All-Immaculate One, and fervently magnify thee; therefore bless me, thy singer, and deliver me from all necessities and sorrows, and preserve me invincible with thy hands.[^6378]

*It is truly meet: Trisagion; after Our Father: troparia and the rest as usual, and dismissal.*[^6379]

*On Saturday at Matins.*[^6380]

*After the 1st Stichologia, Sessional Hymns of the Martyrs, Tone 5:*[^6381]

*Martyrikon 1:* Today the memory of the passion-bearers shines forth, for it hath a radiance even from the heavens; the angelic choir keeps festival, and the human race celebrates with them. Therefore they pray unto the Lord that our souls may find mercy.[^6382]

*Verse: God is marvelous in His saints, the God of Israel.*[^6383]

*Martyrikon 2:* Thou hast granted unto us, O Christ God, the miracles of Thy holy martyrs as an indestructible wall: through their prayers destroy the counsels of the heathen, and establish the sceptres of the kingdom, as Thou art alone good and the Lover of mankind.[^6384]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:* Rejoice, impassable gate of the Lord! Rejoice, wall and protection of those who flee unto thee! Rejoice, storm-free haven, and unwedded one, who hast borne in the flesh thy Creator and God; cease not to pray for those who hymn and worship thy childbirth.[^6385]

*After the 2nd Stichologia, Sessional Hymns of the Cross, Tone 5:*[^6386]

*Martyrikon 1:* Emulating the cup of Thy passions, O Lord, Thy passion-bearers left the beauty of worldly life, and became partakers with the angels. Through their supplications grant unto our souls peace, O Christ God, and great mercy.[^6387]

*Verse: God is marvelous in His saints, the God of Israel.*[^6388]

*Martyrikon 2:* At the triumph of the holy martyrs, the heavenly powers were exceedingly amazed: how in a mortal body, having contended well, they invisibly conquered the incorporeal enemy by the power of the Cross; and they pray unto the Lord that our souls may find mercy.[^6389]

*Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):*[^6390]

*Verse: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken to Thyself, O Lord.*[^6391]

*Sticheron:* Give rest, O our Savior, with the righteous unto Thy servants, and settle them in Thy courts, as it is written, looking past all their transgressions, as Thou art good, voluntary and involuntary, and all those in knowledge and in ignorance, O Lover of mankind.[^6392]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:* O Christ God, Who didst shine forth from the Virgin upon the world, showing forth the sons of light through her, have mercy on us.[^6393]

*Canon to the Holy Martyrs, Hierarchs, Venerables, and the Departed. (Whose acrostic is: Unto Thy servants, O Christ, I offer these songs. Joseph).*[^6394]

*Tone 5. Ode 1. Heirmos:* The land upon which the sun never shone nor saw, the deep which the bare breadth of heaven never saw, Israel traversed undrenched, O Lord: and Thou didst lead him unto the mountain of Thy holiness, praising and singing a song of victory.[^6395]

*Refrain: Holy Martyrs, pray to God for us.*[^6396]

*Troparion 1 (To the Martyrs):* Surrendering your bodies unto harsh judges, ye endured unendurable wounds, O noble sufferers, hoping to receive honors from on high; and Christ led you into eternal mansions, rejoicing and singing a song of victory.[^6397]

*To the Hierarchs:* *Holy Hierarchs of Christ, pray to God for us.*[^6398]

*Troparion 2 (To the Hierarchs):* Venerable, righteous, and glorious hierarchs, having fulfilled the commandments of God, tended the people and guided them well unto the water of understanding; and worthily received torrents of sweetness, gushing forth rivers of healings by grace.[^6399]

*Refrain: All Saints, pray to God for us.*[^6400]

*Troparion 3 (To All Saints):* Guide me, O Lord, unto the havens of life, by the prayers of the most glorious prophets, the all-wise supreme hierarchs, and the holy women who suffered valiantly and trampled the enemy underfoot by fasting with manly mind: who by divine grace appeared unto Thee upon the earth.[^6401]

*Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):* *Give rest, O Lord, to the souls of Thy departed servants.*[^6402]

*Troparion 4 (For the Departed):* We pray to Thee, the propitious God: Give rest in the bosom of Abraham unto Thy servants, O Christ, whom Thou hast translated unto Thyself from worldly turmoil, and make them worthy of eternal light, looking past their transgressions, as Thou art good.[^6403]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:* Rejoice, we cry unto thee, who hast borne Joy, O All-Hymned One: enlighten our minds and souls, and direct all unto the paths of understanding, and pray, O only Bride of God, unto thy Son and God to grant unto all cleansing of transgressions.[^6404]

*Another Canon, for the Departed, sung where there is no Menaion. (Whose acrostic is: Theophanes' fifth canon over the dead).*[^6405]

*Tone 5. Ode 1. Heirmos:* Unto God the Savior, Who guided the people in the sea with dryshod feet, and drowned Pharaoh with all his host, unto Him alone let us sing, for He hath been glorified.[^6406]

*Refrain: God is marvelous in His saints, the God of Israel.*[^6407]

*Troparion 1 (To the Martyrs):* With divine love the passion-bearers of Christ trampled underfoot the pride of the tormentors; and for the departed with faith they ask merciful remission and rest.[^6408]

*Refrain: Give rest, O Lord, to the souls of Thy departed servants.*[^6409]

*Troparion 2 (For the Departed):* In holy habitations, in Thy courts, O Christ, settle the departed: Thou Who didst pour forth Thy most precious blood as a ransom for them, O Master.[^6410]

*Glory:* O Wisdom of God, the express Image, bearing all that is of the Father: those whom Thou hast received, O Compassionate One, do Thou Thyself give rest, granting them ever-existing blessedness.[^6411]

*Both now: Theotokion:* A light-bearing tabernacle, a golden ark holding the divine Word, Who was incarnate for our sake and destroyed the power of death, hast thou appeared, O All-Immaculate One.[^6412]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:* Establish my heart, O Lord, which is tossed by the waves of this life, guiding it unto a quiet haven, as Thou art God.[^6413]

*Refrain: Holy Martyrs, pray to God for us.*[^6414]

*Troparion 1 (To the Martyrs):* Ye spared not your body when beaten with clubs and cut with swords, O all-praised warriors, strengthened by the hope of eternal beauties, O martyrs.[^6415]

*Refrain: Holy Hierarchs of Christ, pray to God for us.*[^6416]

*Troparion 2 (To the Hierarchs):* Ye brightened the vestment of the hierarchal office, having shepherded the flock of Christ upon living pastures with forms of virtue.[^6417]

*Refrain: All Saints, pray to God for us.*[^6418]

*Troparion 3 (To All Saints):* Mortifying the flesh by self-control, the Venerable Ones partook of the divine life; through their sacred prayers, O Christ, deliver us from afflictions.[^6419]

*Refrain: Give rest, O Lord, to the souls of Thy departed servants.*[^6420]

*Troparion 4 (For the Departed):* Grant rest in the world beyond unto the departed, O Lover of mankind, forgiving the debts which they committed on earth, as Thou art good.[^6421]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:* Seeing thee, the Blessed One among women, the choir of women suffered and was brought unto thy Son, O Virgin Mother.[^6422]

*Canon for the Departed. Ode 3. Heirmos:* Establish us, O God our Savior, by Thy power, raise up the horn of Thy Church, which orthodoxly praises Thee.[^6423]

*Troparion 1 (To the Martyrs):* Manfully opposing the fury of the tormentors, the sufferers contended, and they pray unto Christ for the departed.[^6424]

*Troparion 2 (For the Departed):* Having received those who were nurtured by Thy laws and are departed, O Supremely Good One, in Thy radiant habitations, give them rest.[^6425]

*Glory:* O only Merciful God, make those whom Thou hast chosen worthy of holy brightnesses, looking past their transgressions.[^6426]

*Both now: Theotokion:* We hymn thy childbirth, O Pure One, whereby we were delivered from the first condemnation and the curse, and were freed from death.[^6427]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:* I heard, O Lord, the report of Thee, and I was afraid; I understood Thy dispensation, and I glorified Thee, O only Lover of mankind.[^6428]

*Troparion 1 (To the Martyrs):* By Thy power, O Lord, the passion-bearers trampled underfoot the enemy's power, and became strength and great establishment unto the faithful.[^6429]

*Troparion 2 (To the Venerable Ones):* All the Venerable Ones rejoice with joy, and the divine priests are clothed with righteousness as with a vestment.[^6430]

*Troparion 3 (To the Prophets and Women):* Let us all sing of the divinely inspired prophets of God, and let us honor the company of women who finished their course well.[^6431]

*Glory:* Directing toward the haven of Thy kingdom, O Master, give rest unto those whom Thou hast translated from the turmoil and tempest of the present life.[^6432]

*Both now: Theotokion:* The Timeless One became subject to time from thee, O Virgin: entreat Him to loose all the temporal transgressions of my soul.[^6433]

*Canon for the Departed. Ode 4. Heirmos:* I heard, O Lord, of Thy rising from the tomb, and I glorified Thine unconquerable power.[^6434]

*Troparion 1 (To the Martyrs):* Having piously confessed Thee to be co-eternal with the Father, the martyrs were slain, O Christ, and they cry unto Thee: Deliver Thy servants whom Thou hast received.[^6435]

*Troparion 2 (For the Departed):* O Christ, Who alone among the dead didst become free among the dead, granting eternal life unto the dead: give rest now unto Thy departed servants.[^6436]

*Glory:* O Christ, Who camest to save the lost: make those whom Thou hast translated in faith worthy of the habitation of Paradise, having justified them by grace.[^6437]

*Both now: Theotokion:* The power of the Most High overshadowed thee, O Maiden, and made a living Paradise within thee, having in its midst the Tree, the Mediator and Lord.[^6438]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:* Anticipating, have mercy upon my wretched soul that fights by night with the darkness of passions; and cause, O Spiritual Sun, the beams of daytime light to shine forth within me, so as to divide the night from the light.[^6439]

*Troparion 1 (To the Martyrs):* The bones of the martyrs pour forth healing unto the sick; for not being crushed by malice, they renew also our crushings, and all the bones of impiety they ground down like dust.[^6440]

*Troparion 2 (To the Hierarchs and Venerable Ones):* Observing Thy laws, the hierarchs tended the people, and guided them, O Compassionate One, unto the life to come; while the Venerable Ones put to death the tyranny of the passions with a perfected mind.[^6441]

*Troparion 3 (To the Prophets, Righteous, and Women):* Let the prophets be honored, and with them all the righteous through faith; and let those who have lived venerably be praised, and the divine women who were illuminated by torments upon the earth, as servants of Christ.[^6442]

*Glory: Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):* Those whom Thou hast received from the earth, do Thou appoint in heavenly habitations, Thy faithful servants: looking past their transgressions on account of Thy surpassing goodness, O Christ, Who wast incarnate for the sake of us earthly ones.[^6443]

*Both now: Theotokion:* We hymn thee, O Virgin, for whose sake God appeared unto those upon earth, having become man, crying: Rejoice, O fruitful earth, bearing the mystical ear of grain that feeds every breath.[^6444]

*Canon for the Departed. Ode 5. Heirmos:* Rising early, we cry unto Thee, O Lord: Save us! For Thou art our God; besides Thee we know no other.[^6445]

*Troparion 1 (To the Martyrs):* Receiving the supplications of the martyrs, O Lord, number with Thine elect the souls whom Thou hast translated.[^6446]

*Troparion 2 (For the Departed):* Thou hast foretold that those who believe in Thee shall not see death; therefore, O Master, give rest unto the departed.[^6447]

*Glory:* Make Thy servants worthy, O Lord, to obtain the beauty of Thy house, and sweet communion.[^6448]

*Both now: Theotokion:* The Word, Who is co-eternal with the Father, being incarnate of the Virgin, put death to death by His death.[^6449]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:* As Thou didst deliver the prophet from the sea monster, O Lord, so also lead me up, I pray Thee, from the depth of uncontrollable passions, that I may look again toward Thy holy temple.[^6450]

*Troparion 1 (To the Martyrs):* Giving over their bodies unto wounds, unto the hands of the tormentors, the divine martyrs rejoiced in soul; for they truly beheld the divine, ever-existing joy, and divine rewards.[^6451]

*Troparion 2 (To the Hierarchs and Venerable Ones):* Thou art the glory of the all-wise hierarchs and venerable ones, O Christ: by their prayers have mercy on Thy people, whom Thou hast purchased with Thine own blood, as Thou art the Lover of mankind.[^6452]

*Troparion 3 (To the Prophets and Holy Women):* Being enlightened in mind by Thee, O Lord, the prophets clearly foretell distant things as if present; and by Thy power women destroyed the enemy's dominion through suffering and fasting.[^6453]

*Glory:* Numbering Thy departed faithful servants with the choirs of the elect, O Good One, give rest, O Lord, looking past all their transgressions on account of Thy mercy.[^6454]

*Both now: Theotokion:* O Lord Who in the beginning didst create Eve, Thou didst enter into the womb of the Virgin; working our renewal, Thou didst clothe Thyself in the form of a servant, Who art Master of all.[^6455]

*Canon for the Departed. Ode 6. Heirmos:* The abyss surrounded me, the sea-monster became my tomb; but I cried unto Thee, the Lover of mankind, and Thy right hand saved me, O Lord.[^6456]

*Troparion 1 (To the Martyrs):* The host of Thy martyrs, assimilating unto the powers on high, O Christ, entreat Thee: Grant unto the departed the enjoyment that is in Thee, O Lover of mankind.[^6457]

*Troparion 2 (For the Departed):* In places of refreshment, in places of the brightness of saints, in places of relief, O Christ, as Thou art the Lover of mankind, establish Thy servants whom Thou hast received.[^6458]

*Glory:* Cleanse Thy servants, and grant forgiveness of their transgressions, O Lover of mankind, and make them worthy of incorruptible life and blessed inheritance.[^6459]

*Both now: Theotokion:* Who can tell in words the wonder of thy seedless conception, O All-Immaculate One? For thou didst bear God, Who came unto us on account of His tender mercy.[^6460]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:* The prayer of the children was an extinguisher of the fire, the furnace sprinkling with dew was a proclaimer of the miracle, neither scorching nor burning the singers of the God of our fathers.[^6461]

*Troparion 1 (To the Martyrs):* Being kindled by the fire of cruel torments, O glorious martyrs, ye showed a warmest love toward the Lord, which refreshed you with pious contemplation.[^6462]

*Troparion 2 (To the Hierarchs):* Adorned with the wisdom of the Spirit, O all-wise ones, ye were leaders of the people, ministering the gospel of grace; therefore as servants of God we praise you.[^6463]

*Troparion 3 (To the Venerable Ones):* Having put the flesh to death with many afflictions, ye were accounted worthy of the life to come, O Venerable Ones: praying that we, who are put to death by assaults of the passions, may obtain it.[^6464]

*Troparion 4 (To the Prophets and Holy Women):* Let the blessed choir of God's prophets, and the assembly of women, who contended through fasting and suffering and cast down the enemy, be faithfully praised.[^6465]

*Glory:* Having associated with the dead, O Word, the Life of all: direct unto Thy divine haven those who have now left behind the turmoil of life, looking past their transgressions, O Good One.[^6466]

*Both now: Theotokion:* May we who acknowledge thee as the Birthgiver of God with right understanding, be delivered from the eternal fire and darkness by thy mediation, O Lady, and from the invisible enemies that war against us.[^6467]

*Canon for the Departed. Ode 7. Heirmos:* O Thou Who in the fiery furnace didst save the children who sang praise: Blessed is the God of our fathers.[^6468]

*Troparion 1 (To the Martyrs):* Grant now unto the departed to be filled with unapproachable light, O Savior, by the supplications of Thy passion-bearers.[^6469]

*Troparion 2 (For the Departed):* In the Church of the firstborn, O Christ, number those who have fallen asleep in faith, O Compassionate One.[^6470]

*Glory:* Adorned with garments of incorruption, give rest unto Thy servants, O Savior, who have been translated unto Thee.[^6471]

*Both now: Theotokion:* The stream of mortal nature hast thou cut off, O Pure One, having conceived Life without seed, O All-Immaculate One.[^6472]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:* The host of angels, the gathering of men, the King and Creator of all: praise, ye priests; bless, ye levites; supremely exalt, ye people, unto all ages.[^6473]

*Troparion 1 (To the Martyrs):* Having crossed the abyss of torments under the pilotage of the Word, O sufferers, by the shedding of your blood ye drowned all the hosts of the deceiver, and ye live unto the ages.[^6474]

*Troparion 2 (To the Hierarchs):* Having become all-wise sacred celebrants, ye nourished the flock entrusted to you sacredly upon the water of Orthodoxy. Therefore ye enjoy the streams of beautiful sweetness unto the ages.[^6475]

*Troparion 3 (To the Venerable Ones):* Luminaries have ye appeared unto the faithful, O Venerable Ones, having accomplished the works of light, and unto divine[^6476] ...brightness ye have been translated: entreat the Master to deliver us from darkness.[^6477]

*Troparion 4 (To the Holy Women, Prophets, and Righteous):* Rejoice, company of women, who through wounds have received the fullness of all honors; and rejoice, choir of the prophets, and of the righteous who have been well-pleasing unto Christ unto the ages.[^6478]

*Glory: Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):* Grant, O Master, unto Thy servants translated from this life to pass unhindered through the turning sword, settling them within Paradise, as Thou art alone merciful.[^6479]

*Both now: Theotokion:* As the gate leading in unto the divine entrance, as the well-ascended ladder of God, as the unerring guide unto those who are being saved, let us hymn the Virgin Mary, who is full of grace.[^6480]

*Canon for the Departed. Ode 8. Heirmos:* The Son and God Who before the ages was begotten of the Father, and in the latter years was incarnate of the Virgin Mother: praise, ye priests; supremely exalt, ye people, unto all ages.[^6481]

*Troparion 1 (To the Martyrs):* O Thou Who hast accepted the struggles of the holy martyrs, for their sake give rest unto those who have fallen asleep in Thy faith; we praise Thee, O Savior, and supremely exalt Thee unto all ages.[^6482]

*Troparion 2 (For the Departed):* Account worthy those who have been translated from us to be illuminated by the radiance of Thy transcendent glory, as Thou art compassionate, who praise Thee, O Savior, and supremely exalt Thee unto all ages.[^6483]

*Glory:* Appoint with the choirs of the saints those whom Thou hast translated, and number them in the bosom of Abraham with Lazarus, who praise Thee, O Savior, and supremely exalt Thee unto all ages.[^6484]

*Both now: Theotokion:* Adorned with the beauty of purity, O Virgin Mother, having become the dwelling of beneficent splendor: therefore we hymn thee and supremely exalt thee, the pure one, unto all ages.[^6485]

*Then the Canticle of the Theotokos: "My soul doth magnify the Lord."*[^6486]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:* For He that is mighty hath done great things for thee, showing thee forth a pure Virgin after childbirth, as having given birth without seed unto thine own Creator; therefore, O Mother of God, we magnify thee.[^6487]

*Troparion 1 (To the Saints):* Let the sacredly ministrant pastors, the choir of the holy prophets, the innumerable multitude of the righteous, and the assembly of the martyrs be blessed with sacred songs, praying that our souls may be saved.[^6488]

*Troparion 2 (To the Venerable Ones):* As the marvelous assembly of the venerable ones through fasting was all-wisely glorified now by many showings of wonders: through their supplications, O marvelous Lord, make marvelous Thy mercies upon all.[^6489]

*Troparion 3 (To Hieromartyrs and Holy Women):* Together with the hieromartyrs who finished their course well, let the countless multitude of women who suffered and fasted with love be faithfully blessed, abiding with the angelic choirs.[^6490]

*Glory: Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):* The multitude of all the saints entreats Thee, O Word: Give rest unto the multitudes who have been translated from the earth in faith, according to the multitude of Thy mercy, looking past the transgressions committed by them in life.[^6491]

*Both now: Theotokion:* Through mental harm I have committed many sins, O Virgin, and therefore await torment: from which do thou deliver me, who come unto thee with an unhesitating heart, and call upon thy divine protection.[^6492]

*Canon for the Departed. Ode 9. Heirmos:* Thee, the Mother of God beyond mind and word, who in time didst ineffably bear the Timeless One, we the faithful with one accord magnify.[^6493]

*Troparion 1 (To the Martyrs):* Receiving the rewards of sacred sufferings, the martyrs now entreat Thee to grant relief, O Savior, unto those who have been translated in faith.[^6494]

*Troparion 2 (For the Departed):* Thou didst endure mortification, O only immortal Savior, Who grantest resurrection unto the dead as Thou art compassionate, and the brightness of immortality.[^6495]

*Glory:* Thou hast righted us who had fallen into death, and hast taught us to hope for eternal life: which do Thou grant Thy servants to obtain, O Savior.[^6496]

*Both now: Theotokion:* The shadows of the Law passed away by thy childbirth; truth hath shone forth, and grace hath been given, O Mother of God: therefore we magnify thee.[^6497]

*It is truly meet; litany, Exaposteilarion, and the customary Psalms.*[^6498]

*At the Praises, Stichera of the Martyrs, Tone 5:*
*Sticheron 1 (To the Martyrs):* Blessed is the army of the Heavenly King; for even though the passion-bearers were earthborn, yet they hastened to attain the angelic rank. Neglecting their bodies, for the sake of the passions they were accounted worthy of the honor of the Incorporeal Powers: therefore, by their supplications, O Lord, save Thou our souls.[^6499]

...emulating the angelic ranks, O Lord, Thy passion-bearers endured torments as if bodiless, having with one mind the hope and enjoyment of promised good things: by their supplications, O Christ God, have mercy upon us.[^6500]

*Sticheron 2 (To the Martyrs):* The holy martyrs, having contended upon the earth, endured the frost, surrendered themselves unto the fire, and the water received them. Their voice is this: We passed through fire and water, and Thou leddest us out into refreshment.[^6501] By their supplications, O God, have mercy upon us.[^6502]

*Sticheron 3 (To the Martyrs):* While being in torments, the saints rejoicing cried out: These things are commerce for us unto the Master; for instead of the wounds inflicted upon our bodies, a radiant garment shall blossom for us in the resurrection; instead of dishonor, crowns; instead of prison bonds, Paradise; and instead of condemnation with evildoers, dwelling with the angels: by their supplications, O Lord, save Thou our souls.[^6503]

*Hymn for the Departed (Поко́инъ):* Thou Who didst create me, O Lord, and didst lay Thy hand upon me, and commanding me didst say: Unto the earth shalt thou go again; guide me into Thy right path, forgiving me my transgressions; relieve me and save me, I pray Thee, as Thou art the Lover of mankind.[^6504]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:* We call thee blessed, O Virgin Mother of God; for from thee hath shone forth the Sun of righteousness, Christ, Who hath great mercy.[^6505]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera for the Departed, Tone 5.*
*Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice, O container of ascetics" (Ра́дꙋйсѧ):*
*Sticheron 1 (For the Departed):* With the light of Thy countenance, O Christ, enlighten those who have been translated, as Thou art compassionate: settle them in a place of pasture, beside the waters of Thy pure and divine rest; in the longed-for bosom of Abraham the forefather, where Thy light shines purely and the fountains of goodness pour forth; where the assemblies of all the righteous dance brightly, rejoicing in Thy goodness: with them appoint Thy servants, granting them great mercy.[^6506]

*Verse: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken to Thyself, O Lord.*[^6507]
*Sticheron 2 (For the Departed):* To sing with composite voices and to praise Thy dominion, do Thou vouchsafe, O Compassionate One, unto those who have passed from temporal things unto Thee, the Master of all and our God, granting them to be illuminated with Thy beauty, and of Thy sweet and lovely communion...

...most purely to receive and enjoy: where around Thy throne the angels dance and the choirs of the saints joyfully stand about: with them grant rest unto Thy servants, and great mercy.[^6508]

*Verse: Their souls shall dwell among good things.*[^6509]
*Sticheron 3 (For the Departed):* Where the choir of the prophets, the ranks of the apostles and martyrs, and all who from the beginning of the world have been justified by Thy saving Passion and blood, whereby Thou hast redeemed captive man: there give rest unto those who have fallen asleep in faith, as Thou art the Lover of mankind, forgiving their transgressions; for Thou alone didst live upon earth sinless and truly holy, alone free among the dead: therefore grant rest unto Thy servants, and great mercy.[^6510]

*Glory... both now... Theotokion:* From enslavement unto the law of sin hast thou set us free, O Lady, having conceived in thy womb the Lawgiver and King, Christ, O only Virgin Mother, through Whom we are justified freely and by grace: Him do thou now entreat, that the souls of those who acknowledge thee as Mother of God may be written in the Book of Life; so that being saved by thy mediation, O All-Immaculate One, we may receive the beloved redemption of thy Son, worshipping Him Who grants unto the world great mercy.[^6511]

*On Saturday, The Beatitudes, Tone 5:*
*Troparion 1:* The thief on the cross, believing Thee to be God, O Christ, confessed Thee purely from the heart, crying: Remember me, O Lord, in Thy kingdom.[^6512]

*Troparion 2 (To the Martyrs):* Having died, O martyrs, ye destroyed the author-of-evil enemy, and ascended unto the heights, crowned with divine victories, and stood before God, the King of all.[^6513]

*Troparion 3 (To Hierarchs and Venerables):* Being enlightened by the light of the priesthood, the hierarchs were glorified, and the multitude of the venerable obtained life everlasting: therefore they are called blessed.[^6514]

*Troparion 4 (For the Departed):* Settle with the choirs of the elect, in the place of refreshment, O Master, those whom Thou hast translated, O Word: looking past, O Christ, the transgressions committed by them upon earth.[^6515]

*Glory: Triadikon:* O honorable Trinity! Be entreated by the struggles of the venerable hierarchs and honorable martyrs, and grant salvation and great mercy unto the souls of those who have died in faith.[^6516]

*Both now: Theotokion:* O thou who didst contain Him Whom naught can contain, full of the grace of God, and didst bear Him beyond nature and word: do thou, O Lady, entreat Him to be merciful unto all.[^6517]

*[Engraved Tailpiece: An ornamental baroque floral cartouche with foliate scrolls and central rosette, marking the conclusion of Tone 5]*[^6518]

---

### Notes

[^5040]: LITTLE VESPERS (Lord, I have cried, Sticheron 1). First Resurrectional Sticheron at "Lord, I have cried" of Tone 5 Little Vespers, chanted twice according to the rubric; citing 1 Corinthians 15:55-56 and Psalm 9:14 [LXX].
[^5041]: LITTLE VESPERS (Lord, I have cried, Sticheron 2). Second Resurrectional Sticheron at Little Vespers, Tone 5; Isaiah 53:7, Psalm 23:7-9 [LXX], and Isaiah 49:9.
[^5042]: LITTLE VESPERS (Lord, I have cried, Sticheron 3). Third Resurrectional Sticheron at Little Vespers, Tone 5; Psalm 95:7 [LXX].
[^5043]: LITTLE VESPERS (Lord, I have cried, Dogmatic Theotokion). Dogmatic Theotokion of Tone 5 Little Vespers, gathering prophetic types: the closed Eastern Gate (Ezekiel 44:1-2), the Unburnt Bush (Exodus 3:2), and the Mountain uncut by hands (Daniel 2:34-35, 45).
[^5044]: Rubric for Tone 5 Little Vespers prescribing *O Gladsome Light*, Saturday Prokeimenon (Psalm 92:1 [LXX]), and *Vouchsafe, O Lord*, omitting the ektenia before the Aposticha.
[^5045]: LITTLE VESPERS (Aposticha, Resurrection Sticheron, incipit). Opening of the Resurrection Sticheron at the Aposticha of Tone 5 Little Vespers, continued on physical leaf p506 (fol. 504r).
[^5046]: LITTLE VESPERS (Aposticha, Resurrection Sticheron, conclusion). Conclusion of the Resurrection Sticheron at the Aposticha, continued from physical leaf p505 (fol. 503v).
[^5047]: Psalm 44:18 [LXX].
[^5048]: LITTLE VESPERS (Aposticha, Theotokion Prosomoion 1). First Theotokion Prosomoion at the Aposticha of Tone 5 Little Vespers, model *Rejoice, O container of ascetics* (`Ра́дꙋйся по́стникѡвъ`).
[^5049]: Psalm 44:11 [LXX].
[^5050]: LITTLE VESPERS (Aposticha, Theotokion Prosomoion 2). Second Theotokion Prosomoion at the Aposticha, confessing Christ's full humanity (rational soul and free will) and His two energies.
[^5051]: Psalm 44:13 [LXX].
[^5052]: LITTLE VESPERS (Aposticha, Theotokion Prosomoion 3). Third Theotokion Prosomoion at the Aposticha, stringing Patristic typological epithets: beauty of Jacob (Psalm 46:5 [LXX]), flame-bearing tongs (Isaiah 6:6), chariot of the Word, and luminous cloud.
[^5053]: LITTLE VESPERS (Aposticha, Glory & both now, Theotokion). Concluding Theotokion at the Aposticha of Tone 5 Little Vespers, citing Ezekiel 44:2.
[^5054]: Concluding rubric for Tone 5 Little Vespers: Canticle of Simeon (Luke 2:29-32), Trisagion, Sunday Troparion of Tone 5 (*Co-beginningless Word*), Theotokion, and Little Dismissal.
[^5055]: GREAT VESPERS (Lord, I have cried, Sticheron 1). First Resurrectional Sticheron at "Lord, I have cried" of Tone 5 Great Vespers; 1 Corinthians 15:55-56 and Psalm 9:14 [LXX].
[^5056]: GREAT VESPERS (Lord, I have cried, Sticheron 2). Second Resurrectional Sticheron at Great Vespers, Tone 5; Isaiah 53:7, Psalm 23:7-9 [LXX], and Isaiah 49:9.
[^5057]: GREAT VESPERS (Lord, I have cried, Sticheron 3). Third Resurrectional Sticheron at Great Vespers, Tone 5; Psalm 95:7 [LXX].
[^5058]: GREAT VESPERS (Lord, I have cried, Anatolian Sticheron 4). First Anatolian (Eastern) Sticheron at "Lord, I have cried", Tone 5, praising Christ as the Never-Setting Light (*nevecherniy svet*).
[^5059]: GREAT VESPERS (Lord, I have cried, Anatolian Sticheron 5). Second Anatolian Sticheron, Tone 5; Hebrews 2:10 ("Author of salvation").
[^5060]: GREAT VESPERS (Lord, I have cried, Anatolian Sticheron 6, incipit). Opening of the third Anatolian Sticheron at "Lord, I have cried", Tone 5, continued on physical leaf p508 (fol. 506r); Matthew 28:11-15.
[^5061]: GREAT VESPERS (Lord, I have cried, Anatolian Sticheron 6, conclusion). Conclusion of the third Anatolian Sticheron at "Lord, I have cried", continued from physical leaf p507 (fol. 505v); exposing the bribery of the Roman guard and the evidence of the grave clothes (John 20:6-7).
[^5062]: GREAT VESPERS (Lord, I have cried, Anatolian Sticheron 7). Fourth Anatolian Sticheron at "Lord, I have cried", Tone 5.
[^5063]: GREAT VESPERS (Lord, I have cried, Dogmatic Theotokion). The famous Dogmatic Theotokion of Tone 5 Great Vespers (*Въ чермнѣ́мъ мо́ри*), attributed to St. John of Damascus, typologically comparing the Red Sea crossing (Exodus 14) to the Virgin's virginal conception and perpetual virginity.
[^5064]: Rubric for the Little Entrance of Great Vespers, the evening hymn *Phos hilaron*, Prokeimenon, Ektenia, *Vouchsafe, O Lord*, and Evening Litany of Supplication.
[^5065]: GREAT VESPERS (Aposticha, Resurrection Sticheron 1). First Resurrectional Sticheron at the Aposticha of Tone 5 Great Vespers.
[^5066]: Psalm 92:1 [LXX].
[^5067]: GREAT VESPERS (Aposticha, Alphabetical Sticheron 2). Second Aposticha Sticheron (first alphabetical sticheron), Tone 5, contemplating the water and blood from Christ's pierced side (John 19:34).
[^5068]: Psalm 92:1 [LXX].
[^5069]: GREAT VESPERS (Aposticha, Alphabetical Sticheron 3, incipit). Opening of the third Aposticha Sticheron, continued on physical leaf p509 (fol. 507r).
[^5070]: GREAT VESPERS (Aposticha, Alphabetical Sticheron 3, conclusion). Conclusion of the third Aposticha Sticheron, continued from physical leaf p508 (fol. 506v).
[^5071]: Psalm 92:5 [LXX].
[^5072]: GREAT VESPERS (Aposticha, Alphabetical Sticheron 4). Fourth Aposticha Sticheron, Tone 5.
[^5073]: GREAT VESPERS (Aposticha, Glory & both now, Theotokion). Concluding Aposticha Theotokion, Tone 5; Malachi 4:2 ("Sun of Righteousness") and Genesis 1:26-27.
[^5074]: Rubric for the conclusion of Great Vespers: Canticle of Simeon (Luke 2:29-32) and Trisagion prayers.
[^5075]: Troparion (Dismissal Troparion) of the Resurrection, Tone 5.
[^5076]: Dismissal Theotokion of Tone 5; Ezekiel 44:2.
[^5077]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 1, Heirmos). Heirmos of Ode 1, Compline Canon to the Theotokos, Tone 5. Slavonic: *Конѧ̀ и҆ вса́дника въ мо́ре чермно́е...*; Greek: Ἵππον καὶ ἀναβάτην εἰς θάλασσαν Ἐρυθράν; Exodus 15:1-3.
[^5078]: Refrain: Traditional liturgical refrain to the Mother of God throughout the canon.
[^5079]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 1, Troparion 1). First troparion of Ode 1, Tone 5 Compline Canon.
[^5080]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 1, Troparion 2). Second troparion of Ode 1, Tone 5 Compline Canon.
[^5081]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 1, Glory). Doxasticon Troparion (`Сла́ва:`) of Ode 1, Compline Canon to the Theotokos, Tone 5.
[^5082]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 1, Both now). Concluding Troparion (`И҆ ны́нѣ:`) of Ode 1, Tone 5 Compline Canon.
[^5083]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 3, Heirmos). Heirmos of Ode 3, Compline Canon to the Theotokos, Tone 5. Slavonic: *Водрꙋзи́вый на ничесо́мже зе́млю...*; Greek: Ὁ πήξας ἐπ' οὐδενὸς τὴν γῆν; Job 26:7 and Matthew 16:18.
[^5084]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 3, Troparion 1). First troparion of Ode 3, Compline Canon to the Theotokos, Tone 5.
[^5085]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 3, Troparion 2). Second troparion of Ode 3, Compline Canon to the Theotokos, Tone 5; Proverbs 5:22 ("cords of sins").
[^5086]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 3, Glory). Doxasticon Troparion (`Сла́ва:`) of Ode 3, Compline Canon to the Theotokos, Tone 5.
[^5087]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 3, Both now). Concluding Troparion (`И҆ ны́нѣ:`) of Ode 3, Tone 5 Compline Canon; John 4:14.
[^5088]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 4, Heirmos). Heirmos of Ode 4, Compline Canon to the Theotokos, Tone 5. Slavonic: *Бж҃твенное твоѐ разꙋмѣ́въ и҆стоща́нїе...*; Greek: Τὴν θείαν ἐννοήσας σου κένωσιν; Habakkuk 3:13 [LXX] and Philippians 2:7 (*kenosis*).
[^5089]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 4, Troparion 1). First troparion of Ode 4, Compline Canon to the Theotokos, Tone 5.
[^5090]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 4, Troparion 2). Second troparion of Ode 4, Compline Canon to the Theotokos, Tone 5.
[^5091]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 4, Glory). Doxasticon troparion (`Сла́ва:`) of Ode 4, Compline Canon to the Theotokos, Tone 5; citing Psalm 44:10, 14 [LXX] on the royal vesture of the Bride.
[^5092]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 4, Both now). Concluding troparion (`И҆ ны́нѣ:`) of Ode 4, Tone 5 Compline Canon; Luke 1:28 (the Archangel Gabriel's salutation).
[^5093]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 5, Heirmos). Heirmos of Ode 5, Compline Canon to the Theotokos, Tone 5. Slavonic: *Ѡ҆дѣва́йся свѣ́томъ ꙗ҆́кѡ ри́зою...*; Greek: Ὁ ἀναβαλλόμενος φῶς ὡς ἱμάτιον; Psalm 103:2 [LXX].
[^5094]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 5, Troparion 1). First troparion of Ode 5, Tone 5 Compline Canon; Psalm 102:10 [LXX] ("He hath not dealt with us according to our sins").
[^5095]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 5, Troparion 2). Second troparion of Ode 5, Tone 5 Compline Canon; Genesis 1:3 and 2 Corinthians 4:6.
[^5096]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 5, Glory). Doxasticon troparion (`Сла́ва:`) of Ode 5, Compline Canon to the Theotokos, Tone 5; Song of Songs 4:8, 11 (the Bride from Lebanon).
[^5097]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 5, Both now). Concluding troparion (`И҆ ны́нѣ:`) of Ode 5, Tone 5 Compline Canon; 1 Corinthians 1:30 and Galatians 3:13.
[^5098]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 6, Heirmos). Heirmos of Ode 6, Compline Canon to the Theotokos, Tone 5. Slavonic: *Неи́стовствꙋющееся бꙋ́рею дꙋшетлѣ́нною...*; Greek: Μαινομένην κλύδωνι ψυχοφθόρῳ; Jonah 2:3, 7 [LXX].
[^5099]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 6, Troparion 1). First troparion of Ode 6, Tone 5 Compline Canon.
[^5100]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 6, Troparion 2). Second troparion of Ode 6, Tone 5 Compline Canon.
[^5101]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 6, Glory). Doxasticon troparion (`Сла́ва:`) of Ode 6, Compline Canon to the Theotokos, Tone 5.
[^5102]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 6, Both now). Concluding troparion (`И҆ ны́нѣ:`) of Ode 6, Tone 5 Compline Canon; Psalm 118:135 [LXX].
[^5103]: COMPLINE CANON (Sessional Hymn). Sessional Hymn after Ode 6, Tone 5 Compline Canon to the Theotokos.
[^5104]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 7, Heirmos). Heirmos of Ode 7, Compline Canon to the Theotokos, Tone 5. Slavonic: *Превозноси́мый ѻ҆тце́въ Гдⷭ҇ь...*; Greek: Ὁ ὑπερυψούμενος τῶν πατέρων Κύριος; Daniel 3:52-56 [LXX].
[^5105]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 7, Troparion 1). First troparion of Ode 7, Tone 5 Compline Canon; 1 Corinthians 1:24.
[^5106]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 7, Troparion 2). Second troparion of Ode 7, Tone 5 Compline Canon.
[^5107]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 7, Glory). Doxasticon troparion (`Сла́ва:`) of Ode 7, Compline Canon to the Theotokos, Tone 5.
[^5108]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 7, Both now). Concluding troparion (`И҆ ны́нѣ:`) of Ode 7, Tone 5 Compline Canon.
[^5109]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 8, Heirmos). Heirmos of Ode 8, Compline Canon to the Theotokos, Tone 5. Slavonic: *И҆зъ Ѻ҆ц҃а̀ пре́жде вѣ̑къ рожде́ннаго Сы́на и҆ Бг҃а...*; Greek: Τὸν ἐκ Πατρὸς πρὸ αἰώνων γεννηθέντα; Daniel 3:57 [LXX].
[^5110]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 8, Troparion 1). First troparion of Ode 8, Tone 5 Compline Canon.
[^5111]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 8, Troparion 2). Second troparion of Ode 8, Tone 5 Compline Canon.
[^5112]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 8, Glory). Doxasticon troparion (`Сла́ва:`) of Ode 8, Compline Canon to the Theotokos, Tone 5; Exodus 25:31 and Daniel 3:57 [LXX].
[^5113]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 8, Both now). Concluding troparion (`И҆ ны́нѣ:`) of Ode 8, Tone 5 Compline Canon.
[^5114]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 9, Heirmos, incipit). Opening of the Heirmos of Ode 9, Compline Canon to the Theotokos, Tone 5, continued on physical leaf p513 (fol. 511r); Slavonic: *И҆са́їе ликꙋ́й, Дѣ́ва и҆мѣ̀ въ чре́вѣ...*; Greek: Ἡσαΐα χόρευε; Isaiah 7:14; Matthew 1:23.
[^5115]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 9, Heirmos, conclusion). Conclusion of the Heirmos of Ode 9, continued from physical leaf p512 (fol. 510v); Zechariah 6:12; Luke 1:78. Note: the printed running folio header on fol. 511r erroneously reads `Гла́съ д҃.` in place of `Гла́съ є҃.`
[^5116]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 9, Troparion 1). First troparion of Ode 9, Tone 5 Compline Canon.
[^5117]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 9, Troparion 2). Second troparion of Ode 9, Tone 5 Compline Canon.
[^5118]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 9, Glory). Doxasticon troparion (`Сла́ва:`) of Ode 9, Tone 5 Compline Canon. A strong defense of the veneration of holy icons against iconoclasm; the 1895 Lviv print explicitly isolates the clause `[всѧ́кѡ ненави́дѧще є҆реті́къ безꙋ́мїе на землѝ]` in square brackets.
[^5119]: COMPLINE CANON (Ode 9, Both now). Concluding troparion (`И҆ ны́нѣ:`) of Ode 9, Tone 5 Compline Canon.
[^5120]: Rubric concluding Saturday Compline: Megalynarion (*It is truly meet*), Trisagion prayers, troparia, and dismissal.
[^5121]: SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE (Canon to the Holy Trinity, Tone 5). Title and acrostic heading for the Sunday Midnight Office Canon, composed by St. Metrophanes of Smyrna with the acrostic: *Пра́вило пѧ́тое Свѣ́тꙋ трисо́лнечномꙋ* (Greek: *Κανὼν πέμπτος τριφαεῖ φάει*).
[^5122]: SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE (Trinity Canon, Ode 1, Heirmos). Heirmos of Ode 1, Sunday Midnight Office Trinity Canon, Tone 5. Slavonic: *Конѧ̀ и҆ вса́дника въ мо́ре чермно́е...*; Greek: Ἵππον καὶ ἀναβάτην εἰς θάλασσαν Ἐρυθράν; Exodus 15:1-3.
[^5123]: Refrain: Liturgical refrain for the Sunday Midnight Office Canon to the Holy Trinity.
[^5124]: SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE (Trinity Canon, Ode 1, Troparion 1). First troparion of Ode 1, Tone 5 Trinity Canon.
[^5125]: SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE (Trinity Canon, Ode 1, Troparion 2). Second troparion of Ode 1, Tone 5 Trinity Canon; Isaiah 6:3.
[^5126]: SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE (Trinity Canon, Ode 1, Glory). Doxasticon troparion (`Сла́ва:`) of Ode 1, Tone 5 Trinity Canon.
[^5127]: SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE (Trinity Canon, Ode 1, Theotokion, incipit). Opening of the concluding Theotokion (`Бг҃оро́диченъ`) of Ode 1, continued on physical leaf p514 (fol. 512r); Psalm 71:6 [LXX]; Judges 6:37-40.
[^5128]: SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE (Trinity Canon, Ode 1, Theotokion, conclusion). Conclusion of the Theotokion of Ode 1, continued from physical leaf p513 (fol. 511v).
[^5129]: SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE (Trinity Canon, Ode 3, Heirmos). Heirmos of Ode 3, Sunday Midnight Office Trinity Canon, Tone 5. Slavonic: *Водрꙋзи́вый на ничесо́мже зе́млю...*; Greek: Ὁ πήξας ἐπ' οὐδενὸς τὴν γῆν; Job 26:7; Matthew 16:18.
[^5130]: SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE (Trinity Canon, Ode 3, Troparion 1). First troparion of Ode 3, Tone 5 Trinity Canon.

[^5132]: SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE (Trinity Canon, Ode 3, Glory). Doxasticon troparion (`Сла́ва:`) of Ode 3, Tone 5 Trinity Canon.
[^5133]: SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE (Trinity Canon, Ode 3, Theotokion). Concluding Theotokion (`Бг҃оро́диченъ`) of Ode 3, Tone 5 Trinity Canon; Exodus 3:2; Isaiah 9:6 [LXX].
[^5134]: SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE (Sessional Hymn, Tone 5). Sessional Hymn after Ode 3, Sunday Midnight Office Trinity Canon, Tone 5, model *The Co-beginningless Word* (`Собезначáлное Сло́во`).
[^5135]: SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE (Sessional Theotokion, Tone 5). Concluding Theotokion (`Бг҃оро́диченъ`) after Ode 3, Tone 5.
[^5136]: SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE (Trinity Canon, Ode 4, Heirmos). Heirmos of Ode 4, Sunday Midnight Office Trinity Canon, Tone 5. Slavonic: *Бж҃твенное твоѐ разꙋмѣ́въ и҆стоща́нїе...*; Greek: Τὴν θείαν ἐννοήσας σου κένωσιν; Habakkuk 3:13 [LXX]; Philippians 2:7.

[^5139]: SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE (Trinity Canon, Ode 4, Glory). Doxasticon troparion (`Сла́ва:`) of Ode 4, Tone 5 Trinity Canon.

[^5141]: SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE (Trinity Canon, Ode 5, Heirmos). Heirmos of Ode 5, Sunday Midnight Office Trinity Canon, Tone 5. Slavonic: *Ѡ҆дѣва́йся свѣ́томъ ꙗ҆́кѡ ри́зою...*; Greek: Ὁ ἀναβαλλόμενος φῶς ὡς ἱμάτιον; Psalm 103:2 [LXX].

[^5146]: SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE (Trinity Canon, Ode 6, Heirmos). Heirmos of Ode 6, Sunday Midnight Office Trinity Canon, Tone 5. Slavonic: *Неи́стовствꙋющееся бꙋ́рею дꙋшетлѣ́нною...*; Greek: Μαινομένην κλύδωνι ψυχοφθόρῳ; Jonah 2:3, 7 [LXX].

[^5154]: SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE (Trinity Canon, Ode 7, Heirmos). Heirmos of Ode 7, Sunday Midnight Office Trinity Canon, Tone 5. Slavonic: *Превозноси́мый ѻ҆тце́въ Гдⷭ҇ь...*; Greek: Ὁ ὑπερυψούμενος τῶν πατέρων Κύριος; Daniel 3:52-56 [LXX].

[^5159]: SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE (Trinity Canon, Ode 8, Heirmos). Heirmos of Ode 8, Sunday Midnight Office Trinity Canon, Tone 5. Slavonic: *Тебѣ̀ Вседѣ́телю, въ пещѝ ѻ҆́троцы...*; Greek: Σοὶ τῷ Παντουργῷ; Daniel 3:57 [LXX].
[^5160]: SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE (Trinity Canon, Ode 8, Troparion 1, incipit). Opening of the first troparion of Ode 8, continued on physical leaf p517 (fol. 515r); Genesis 18:1-3 (the Hospitality of Abraham).
[^5161]: SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE (Trinity Canon, Ode 8, Troparion 2). Second troparion of Ode 8, Tone 5 Trinity Canon; 1 Timothy 6:16.
[^5162]: SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE (Trinity Canon, Ode 8, Glory). Doxasticon troparion (`Сла́ва:`) of Ode 8, Tone 5 Trinity Canon, expressing classical Eastern patristic pneumatology: procession from the Father and manifestation through the Son (*Сы́номъ же ꙗ҆влѧ́емь* / *δι' Υἱοῦ ἐκφαινομένου*).
[^5163]: SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE (Trinity Canon, Ode 8, Theotokion). Concluding Theotokion (`Бг҃оро́диченъ`) of Ode 8, Tone 5 Trinity Canon.
[^5164]: SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE (Trinity Canon, Ode 9, Heirmos). Heirmos of Ode 9, Sunday Midnight Office Trinity Canon, Tone 5. Slavonic: *И҆са́їе ликꙋ́й, Дѣ́ва и҆мѣ̀ въ чре́вѣ...*; Greek: Ἡσαΐα χόρευε; Isaiah 7:14; Zechariah 6:12; Luke 1:78.

[^5166]: SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE (Trinity Canon, Ode 9, Troparion 2). Second troparion of Ode 9, Tone 5 Trinity Canon; Isaiah 6:2-3.
[^5167]: SUNDAY MIDNIGHT OFFICE (Trinity Canon, Ode 9, Glory). Doxasticon troparion (`Сла́ва:`) of Ode 9, Tone 5 Trinity Canon; Isaiah 6:1-3.

[^5169]: Rubric for the conclusion of Sunday Midnight Office: Triadika of St. Gregory of Sinai (*It is meet in truth to hymn the transcendent Trinity*), Megalynarion, Trisagion, and dismissal.
[^5170]: SUNDAY MATINS (Opening Rubric, Tone 5). Rubric for *God is the Lord* (Psalm 117:27 [LXX]), Resurrection Troparion *The Co-beginningless Word*, Menaion Troparion, Theotokion, and Kathisma readings from the Psalter.
[^5171]: SUNDAY MATINS (Kathisma 1, Resurrection Sessional Hymn 1, incipit). First Resurrection Sessional Hymn after the First Kathisma, Tone 5, continued on physical leaf p518 (fol. 516r).
[^5172]: Psalm 9:33 [LXX] / 10:12 [MT].
[^5173]: SUNDAY MATINS (Kathisma 1, Resurrection Sessional Hymn 2). Second Resurrection Sessional Hymn after the First Kathisma, Tone 5; Peter's repentance contrasting with the Myrrh-bearers and the Good Thief (Luke 22:54-62; Luke 23:42-43; Matthew 4:19).
[^5174]: SUNDAY MATINS (Kathisma 1, Sessional Theotokion). Concluding Theotokion after the First Kathisma, Tone 5.
[^5175]: SUNDAY MATINS (Kathisma 2, Resurrection Sessional Hymn 1). First Resurrection Sessional Hymn after the Second Kathisma, Tone 5; Matthew 27:65-66.
[^5176]: Psalm 9:2 [LXX].
[^5177]: SUNDAY MATINS (Kathisma 2, Resurrection Sessional Hymn 2). Second Resurrection Sessional Hymn after the Second Kathisma, Tone 5; John 19:34 and Matthew 28:8.
[^5178]: SUNDAY MATINS (Kathisma 2, Sessional Theotokion). Concluding Theotokion after the Second Kathisma, Tone 5; Ezekiel 44:2 and Exodus 3:2.
[^5179]: SUNDAY MATINS (Polyeleos Rubric). Prescribing Psalm 118 [LXX] (*Blessed are the undefiled*) or the Polyeleos, the Resurrectional Evlogitaria (*The assembly of the angels was amazed*), and the Little Ektenia.
[^5180]: SUNDAY MATINS (Hypakoe, Tone 5). Resurrection Hypakoe of Tone 5; Mark 16:6-7, 20.
[^5181]: SUNDAY MATINS (Anavathmoi, Antiphon 1, Verse 1). First Antiphon of the Hymns of Degrees, Tone 5, Verse 1; Psalm 119:1-2 [LXX].
[^5182]: SUNDAY MATINS (Anavathmoi, Antiphon 1, Verse 2). First Antiphon of the Hymns of Degrees, Tone 5, Verse 2; the blessedness of the eremitic life.
[^5183]: SUNDAY MATINS (Anavathmoi, Antiphon 1, Glory). Trinitarian-Pneumatological troparion (`Сла́ва:`) of Antiphon 1, Tone 5.
[^5184]: Liturgical repetition of the Pneumatikon troparion for *Both now*.
[^5185]: SUNDAY MATINS (Anavathmoi, Antiphon 2, Verse 1). Second Antiphon of the Hymns of Degrees, Tone 5, Verse 1; Psalm 120:1-2 [LXX].
[^5186]: SUNDAY MATINS (Anavathmoi, Antiphon 2, Verse 2). Second Antiphon of the Hymns of Degrees, Tone 5, Verse 2; Psalm 120:5, 7 [LXX].
[^5187]: SUNDAY MATINS (Anavathmoi, Antiphon 2, Glory). Trinitarian-Pneumatological troparion (`Сла́ва:`) of Antiphon 2, Tone 5.
[^5188]: Liturgical repetition of the Pneumatikon troparion for *Both now*.
[^5189]: SUNDAY MATINS (Anavathmoi, Antiphon 3, Verse 1). Third Antiphon of the Hymns of Degrees, Tone 5, Verse 1; Psalm 121:1 [LXX].
[^5190]: SUNDAY MATINS (Anavathmoi, Antiphon 3, Verse 2). Third Antiphon of the Hymns of Degrees, Tone 5, Verse 2; Psalm 121:5 [LXX].
[^5191]: SUNDAY MATINS (Anavathmoi, Antiphon 3, Glory). Trinitarian-Pneumatological troparion (`Сла́ва:`) of Antiphon 3, Tone 5.
[^5192]: Liturgical repetition of the Pneumatikon troparion for *Both now*.
[^5193]: SUNDAY MATINS (Sunday Prokeimenon, Tone 5). Sunday Prokeimenon (Psalm 9:33 [LXX]), Matins Gospel order, *Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ*, and Psalm 50 [LXX].
[^5194]: SUNDAY MATINS (Resurrection Canon, Ode 1, Heirmos). Heirmos of Ode 1, Sunday Resurrection Canon, Tone 5. Slavonic: *Конѧ̀ и҆ вса́дника въ мо́ре чермно́е...*; Greek: Ἵππον καὶ ἀναβάτην εἰς θάλασσαν Ἐρυθράν; Exodus 15:1-3.
[^5195]: Refrain: Liturgical refrain for the Sunday Resurrection Canon, Tone 5.
[^5196]: SUNDAY MATINS (Resurrection Canon, Ode 1, Troparion 1). First troparion of Ode 1, Tone 5 Resurrection Canon; Matthew 27:29 and Genesis 3:18 (the crown of thorns).
[^5197]: SUNDAY MATINS (Resurrection Canon, Ode 1, Troparion 2). Second troparion of Ode 1, Tone 5 Resurrection Canon; Psalm 39:3 [LXX] and Song of Songs 1:3.
[^5198]: SUNDAY MATINS (Resurrection Canon, Ode 1, Theotokion). Concluding Theotokion (`Бг҃оро́диченъ`) of Ode 1, Tone 5 Resurrection Canon; Luke 1:28, 42 and Galatians 3:13.
[^5199]: SUNDAY MATINS (Second Canon, Ode 1, Heirmos). Heirmos of Ode 1, Second Canon of the Cross and Resurrection, Tone 5. Slavonic: *Спаси́телю Бг҃ꙋ, въ мо́ри лю́ди немо́крыми нога́ми...*; Greek: Τῷ Σωτῆρι Θεῷ; Exodus 14:21-28, 15:1.
[^5200]: SUNDAY MATINS (Second Canon, Ode 1, Troparion 1). First troparion of Ode 1, Tone 5 Second Canon; Genesis 3:6, 17 and Colossians 2:14.
[^5201]: SUNDAY MATINS (Second Canon, Ode 1, Troparion 2). Second troparion of Ode 1, Tone 5 Second Canon; Ephesians 2:6 and Hebrews 1:3.
[^5202]: SUNDAY MATINS (Second Canon, Ode 1, Theotokion). Concluding Theotokion (`Бг҃оро́диченъ`) of Ode 1, Tone 5 Second Canon; John 1:18.
[^5203]: SUNDAY MATINS (Third Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 1, Troparion 1). First troparion of Ode 1, Tone 5 Third Canon to the Theotokos (Acrostic letter 1: `С`, *Свѣ́тъ...*); John 8:12.
[^5204]: SUNDAY MATINS (Third Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 1, Troparion 2). Second troparion of Ode 1, Tone 5 Third Canon to the Theotokos (Acrostic letter 2: `в`, *...всѧ́ческаѧ ѹ҆добри́вшаго*).
[^5205]: SUNDAY MATINS (Third Canon to the Theotokos, Ode 1, Troparion 3). Third troparion of Ode 1, Tone 5 Third Canon to the Theotokos (Acrostic letter 3: `ѣ`, *...Тебѐ*); Exodus 3:2.
[^5206]: SUNDAY MATINS (Ode 3, Katavasia). Opening words of the Katavasia incipit of Ode 3, Sunday Matins, Tone 5; Slavonic: *Катава́сіа: Ѿве́рзꙋ оу҆ста̀ моѧ̑...*; taken from the Heirmologion (Canon of the Akathist to the Theotokos, Tone 4).
[^5207]: RESURRECTION CANON (Ode 3, Heirmos). Heirmos of Ode 3, Sunday Matins, Tone 5; Slavonic: *Водрꙋзи́вый на ничесо́мже зе́млю...*; Greek: Ὁ πήξας ἐπ' οὐδενὸς τὴν γῆν τῷ προστάγματί σου; Job 26:7.
[^5208]: RESURRECTION CANON (Ode 3, Troparion 1). First troparion of Ode 3; Deuteronomy 32:13; Psalm 68:22 [LXX]; Matthew 27:34, 48.
[^5209]: RESURRECTION CANON (Ode 3, Troparion 2). Second troparion of Ode 3; Exodus 13:21; Acts 2:1-4.
[^5210]: RESURRECTION CANON (Ode 3, Theotokion). Concluding Theotokion of Ode 3; John 1:14.

[^5223]: CANON OF THE CROSS AND RESURRECTION (Ode 4, Heirmos). Heirmos of Ode 4, second canon, Tone 5; Slavonic: *Оу҆слы́шахъ слꙋ́хъ си́лы креста̀...*; Greek: Εἰσακήκοα τὴν ἀκοὴν τῆς δυναστείας τοῦ Σταυροῦ σου; Habakkuk 3:2; Luke 23:43.

[^5228]: CANON TO THE THEOTOKOS (Ode 4, Troparion 2). Second troparion of Ode 4, third canon; Hebrews 11:3.

[^5232]: RESURRECTION CANON (Ode 5, Heirmos). Heirmos of Ode 5, Sunday Matins, Tone 5; Slavonic: *Ѡ҆дѣѧ́йсѧ свѣ́томъ ꙗ҆́кѡ ри́зою...*; Greek: Ὁ ἀναβαλλόμενος φῶς ὡς ἱμάτιον; Psalm 103:2 [LXX]; Isaiah 26:9.

[^5235]: RESURRECTION CANON (Ode 5, Theotokion). Concluding Theotokion of Ode 5; Jeremiah 23:6; 1 Corinthians 1:30.
[^5236]: CANON OF THE CROSS AND RESURRECTION (Ode 5, Heirmos). Heirmos of Ode 5, second canon, Tone 5; Slavonic: *Оу҆тре́нююще вопіе́мъ тѝ Гдⷭ҇и...*; Greek: Ὀρθρίζοντες βοῶμέν σοι Κύριε; Isaiah 26:9, 13.

[^5239]: CANON OF THE CROSS AND RESURRECTION (Ode 5, Troparion 3). Third troparion of Ode 5, second canon.
[^5240]: CANON OF THE CROSS AND RESURRECTION (Ode 5, Theotokion). Concluding Theotokion of Ode 5, second canon; John 1:1, 14.

[^5242]: CANON TO THE THEOTOKOS (Ode 5, Troparion 2). Second troparion of Ode 5, third canon; Psalm 71:6 [LXX]; Isaiah 45:8.

[^5245]: RESURRECTION CANON (Ode 6, Troparion 1). First troparion of Ode 6; Genesis 3:6.
[^5246]: RESURRECTION CANON (Ode 6, Troparion 2). Second troparion of Ode 6; 1 Peter 3:18-19.
[^5247]: RESURRECTION CANON (Ode 6, Theotokion). Concluding Theotokion of Ode 6; Hebrews 1:3.
[^5248]: CANON OF THE CROSS AND RESURRECTION (Ode 6, Heirmos). Heirmos of Ode 6, second canon, Tone 5; Slavonic: *Ѡ҆би́де мѧ̀ бе́здна...*; Greek: Περιέσχε με ἄβυσσος; Jonah 2:6.
[^5249]: CANON OF THE CROSS AND RESURRECTION (Ode 6, Troparion 1). First troparion of Ode 6, second canon; Isaiah 65:2; John 11:52.
[^5250]: CANON OF THE CROSS AND RESURRECTION (Ode 6, Troparion 2). Second troparion of Ode 6, second canon; Psalm 106:16 [LXX].

[^5252]: CANON TO THE THEOTOKOS (Ode 6, Troparion 1). First troparion of Ode 6, third canon.
[^5253]: CANON TO THE THEOTOKOS (Ode 6, Troparion 2). Second troparion of Ode 6, third canon.
[^5254]: CANON TO THE THEOTOKOS (Ode 6, Troparion 3). Third troparion of Ode 6, third canon; Hebrews 5:9.
[^5255]: SUNDAY KONTAKION (Tone 5, incipit). Opening of the Sunday Resurrection Kontakion, Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (*Собезнача́льное Сло́во*), continued on physical leaf p525 (fol. 523r misprinted `ф҃кг`); Greek: Τὸν συνάναρχον Λόγον; 1 Corinthians 15:55-57.
[^5256]: SUNDAY KONTAKION (Tone 5, conclusion). Conclusion of the Sunday Resurrection Kontakion, Tone 5, continued from physical leaf p524 (fol. 522v).
[^5257]: SUNDAY IKOS (Tone 5). Resurrection Ikos, Tone 5; Matthew 28:5-10; Mark 16:6-7; John 16:33.
[^5258]: RESURRECTION CANON (Ode 7, Heirmos). Heirmos of Ode 7, Sunday Matins, Tone 5; Slavonic: *Превозноси́мый ѻ҆тце́въ Гдⷭ҇ь...*; Greek: Ὁ ὑπερυψούμενος τῶν Πατέρων Κύριος; Daniel 3:26, 49-50.
[^5259]: RESURRECTION CANON (Ode 7, Troparion 1). First troparion of Ode 7; Job 40:20 [LXX]; Genesis 3:15; Hebrews 2:14.
[^5260]: RESURRECTION CANON (Ode 7, Troparion 2). Second troparion of Ode 7; Job 38:4-6; Matthew 27:59-60.
[^5261]: RESURRECTION CANON (Ode 7, Theotokion). Concluding Theotokion of Ode 7; Chalcedonian definition of the two natures united in the one divine Hypostasis of the Word.
[^5262]: CANON OF THE CROSS AND RESURRECTION (Ode 7, Heirmos). Heirmos of Ode 7, second canon, Tone 5; Slavonic: *Въ пещѝ ѻ҆́гненнѣ пѣсносло́вцы...*; Greek: Ὁ ἐν καμίνῳ πυρὸς τοὺς ὑμνολόγους σώσας Παῖδας; Daniel 3:23-28.
[^5263]: CANON OF THE CROSS AND RESURRECTION (Ode 7, Troparion 1). First troparion of Ode 7, second canon; Colossians 2:14-15.
[^5264]: CANON OF THE CROSS AND RESURRECTION (Ode 7, Troparion 2). Second troparion of Ode 7, second canon; Matthew 27:52-53.
[^5265]: CANON OF THE CROSS AND RESURRECTION (Ode 7, Troparion 3). Third troparion of Ode 7, second canon; Hebrews 2:14.
[^5266]: CANON OF THE CROSS AND RESURRECTION (Ode 7, Theotokion). Concluding Theotokion of Ode 7, second canon; Luke 1:43.
[^5267]: CANON TO THE THEOTOKOS (Ode 7, Troparion 1). First troparion of Ode 7, third canon; Philippians 2:6-7.
[^5268]: CANON TO THE THEOTOKOS (Ode 7, Troparion 2, incipit). Opening of the second troparion of Ode 7, third canon, continued on physical leaf p526 (fol. 524r); Isaiah 66:1.
[^5269]: CANON TO THE THEOTOKOS (Ode 7, Troparion 2, conclusion). Conclusion of the second troparion of Ode 7, continued from physical leaf p525 (fol. 523v).
[^5270]: CANON TO THE THEOTOKOS (Ode 7, Troparion 3). Third troparion of Ode 7, third canon; Luke 1:35.
[^5271]: CANON TO THE THEOTOKOS (Ode 7, Troparion 4). Fourth troparion of Ode 7, third canon; Psalm 103:2 [LXX].
[^5272]: RESURRECTION CANON (Ode 8, Heirmos). Heirmos of Ode 8, Sunday Matins, Tone 5; Slavonic: *Тебѣ̀ Вседѣ́телю, въ пещѝ ѻ҆́троцы...*; Greek: Σοὶ τῷ Παντουργῷ ἐν τῇ καμίνῳ Παῖδες; Daniel 3:57.
[^5273]: RESURRECTION CANON (Ode 8, Troparion 1). First troparion of Ode 8; Matthew 26:39; doctrine of the two natural wills and operations (Dyothelitism) confirmed at the Sixth Ecumenical Council (Constantinople III, 680–681).
[^5274]: RESURRECTION CANON (Ode 8, Troparion 2). Second troparion of Ode 8; 1 Peter 3:19; Hosea 13:14.
[^5275]: RESURRECTION CANON (Ode 8, Theotokion). Concluding Theotokion of Ode 8; theandric conception beyond comprehension.
[^5276]: CANON OF THE CROSS AND RESURRECTION (Ode 8, Heirmos). Heirmos of Ode 8, second canon, Tone 5; Slavonic: *И҆зъ Ѻ҆ц҃а̀ пре́жде вѣ̑къ рожде́ннаго...*; Greek: Τὸν ἐκ Πατρὸς πρὸ αἰώνων γεννηθέντα; Daniel 3:57; Galatians 4:4.
[^5277]: CANON OF THE CROSS AND RESURRECTION (Ode 8, Troparion 1). First troparion of Ode 8, second canon; John 12:32.
[^5278]: CANON OF THE CROSS AND RESURRECTION (Ode 8, Theotokion). Concluding Theotokion of Ode 8, second canon; Psalm 79:2 [LXX]; Ezekiel 10:1-22.
[^5279]: CANON TO THE THEOTOKOS (Ode 8, Troparion 1). First troparion of Ode 8, third canon; Genesis 3:16.
[^5280]: CANON TO THE THEOTOKOS (Ode 8, Troparion 2). Second troparion of Ode 8, third canon; Luke 2:13-14.
[^5281]: CANON TO THE THEOTOKOS (Ode 8, Troparion 3). Third troparion of Ode 8, third canon; Revelation 22:1.
[^5282]: CANON TO THE THEOTOKOS (Ode 8, Troparion 4). Fourth troparion of Ode 8, third canon; Exodus 24:12; 2 Corinthians 3:3.
[^5283]: SUNDAY MATINS RUBRIC (Magnificat). Rubric for singing the Canticle of the Theotokos ("My soul magnifies the Lord") with the Megalynarion refrain: "More honorable than the Cherubim"; Luke 1:46-55.
[^5284]: RESURRECTION CANON (Ode 9, Heirmos). Heirmos of Ode 9, Sunday Matins, Tone 5; Slavonic: *И҆са́їа ликꙋ́й, Дѣ́ва и҆мѣ̀ въ чре́вѣ...*; Greek: Ἡσαΐα χόρευε; Isaiah 7:14; Matthew 1:23; Zechariah 6:12; Luke 1:78.
[^5285]: RESURRECTION CANON (Ode 9, Troparion 1). First troparion of Ode 9; Hebrews 2:14-17; 4:15.
[^5286]: RESURRECTION CANON (Ode 9, Troparion 2). Second troparion of Ode 9; John 19:34; Hebrews 9:11-14.
[^5287]: RESURRECTION CANON (Ode 9, Theotokion). Concluding Theotokion of Ode 9; Chalcedonian Christological confession of true God and true man.
[^5288]: CANON OF THE CROSS AND RESURRECTION (Ode 9, Heirmos). Heirmos of Ode 9, second canon, Tone 5; Slavonic: *Тѧ̀ па́че оу҆ма̀ и҆ словесѐ Ма́терь Бж҃ію...*; Greek: Σὲ τὴν ὑπὲρ νοῦν καὶ λόγον Μητέρα Θεοῦ.
[^5289]: CANON OF THE CROSS AND RESURRECTION (Ode 9, Troparion 1). First troparion of Ode 9, second canon; 1 Corinthians 1:18, 23-24.
[^5290]: CANON OF THE CROSS AND RESURRECTION (Ode 9, Troparion 2). Second troparion of Ode 9, second canon; 1 Corinthians 15:4, 20.
[^5291]: CANON OF THE CROSS AND RESURRECTION (Ode 9, Theotokion). Concluding Theotokion of Ode 9, second canon; 1 John 2:1-2.
[^5292]: CANON TO THE THEOTOKOS (Ode 9, Troparion 1). First troparion of Ode 9, third canon; Job 10:10; Luke 1:35; John 1:14.
[^5293]: CANON TO THE THEOTOKOS (Ode 9, Troparion 2). Second troparion of Ode 9, third canon; 1 Corinthians 15:54-55.
[^5294]: CANON TO THE THEOTOKOS (Ode 9, Troparion 3). Third troparion of Ode 9, third canon; Psalm 44:10, 14 [LXX].
[^5295]: CANON TO THE THEOTOKOS (Ode 9, Troparion 4). Fourth troparion of Ode 9, third canon; Luke 1:34-35, 48.
[^5296]: SUNDAY MATINS RUBRIC (Little Ektenia). Rubric prescribing the Little Ektenia after the Katavasia of Ode 9.
[^5297]: SUNDAY MATINS RUBRIC (Exapostilarion). Rubric for "Holy is the Lord our God" (Psalm 98:9 [LXX]) and the appointed Resurrectional Exapostilarion (Morning Hymn of Light).
[^5298]: SUNDAY MATINS (The Praises, Title). Rubric and genre title for the Resurrection Stichera at the Praises (*На хвали́тѣхъ*), Tone 5; Psalms 148, 149, 150.
[^5299]: SUNDAY MATINS (The Praises, Sticheron 1). First Resurrection Sticheron at the Praises, Tone 5; Matthew 27:66; 28:2-4; John 20:19.
[^5300]: SUNDAY MATINS (The Praises, Sticheron 2). Second Resurrection Sticheron at the Praises, Tone 5; Psalm 106:16 [LXX]; John 20:5-7; Matthew 28:7, 10.
[^5301]: SUNDAY MATINS (The Praises, Sticheron 3). Third Resurrection Sticheron at the Praises, Tone 5; Matthew 28:1-7; Mark 16:1-6.
[^5302]: SUNDAY MATINS (The Praises, Sticheron 4). Fourth Resurrection Sticheron at the Praises, Tone 5; John 20:19-20, 26-27; Romans 1:3.
[^5303]: SUNDAY MATINS (The Praises, Anatolian Stichera Title). Heading for the Anatolian (Eastern) Stichera (*Востѡ́чны стїхи̑ры*), attributed to Anatolius of Constantinople.
[^5304]: SUNDAY MATINS (The Praises, Sticheron 5). First Anatolian Sticheron at the Praises, Tone 5; 1 Timothy 1:17; Isaiah 45:5.
[^5305]: SUNDAY MATINS (The Praises, Sticheron 6). Second Anatolian Sticheron at the Praises, Tone 5; Luke 23:43; Psalm 106:16 [LXX].
[^5306]: SUNDAY MATINS (The Praises, Psalm Verse 1). Psalm verse appointed before the seventh Sticheron; Psalm 9:33 [LXX: `Воскреснѝ Гдⷭ҇и Бж҃е мо́й, да вознесе́тсѧ рꙋка̀ твоѧ̀; не забꙋ́ди оу҆бо́гихъ твои́хъ до конца̀`].
[^5307]: SUNDAY MATINS (The Praises, Sticheron 7). Third Anatolian Sticheron at the Praises, Tone 5; Mark 16:1-8; Luke 24:1-10.
[^5308]: SUNDAY MATINS (The Praises, Psalm Verse 2). Psalm verse appointed before the eighth Sticheron; Psalm 9:2 [LXX: `И҆сповѣ́мсѧ тебѣ̀ Гдⷭ҇и, всѣ́мъ се́рдцемъ мои́мъ, повѣ́мъ всѧ̑ чꙋдеса̀ твоѧ̑`].
[^5309]: SUNDAY MATINS (The Praises, Sticheron 8). Fourth Anatolian Sticheron at the Praises, Tone 5; dramatic address to the guards and Jewish authorities regarding the empty tomb; Matthew 28:2-4, 11-15; 1 Corinthians 15:55.
[^5310]: SUNDAY MATINS RUBRIC (Glory... Evangelical Sticheron). Rubric directing the singing of the appointed Morning Evangelical Sticheron (`стїхи́ра оу҆́треннаѧ є҆ѵⷢлскаѧ`) according to the eleven-week Eothina cycle.
[^5311]: SUNDAY MATINS RUBRIC (Both now... Theotokion). Rubric for the Sunday Theotokion at the Praises: "Most blessed art thou, O Virgin Theotokos..." (*Преблагослове́нна є҆сѝ, Бц҃е Дѣ́во*).
[^5312]: SUNDAY MATINS RUBRIC (Great Doxology and Dismissal). Concluding rubrics of Sunday Matins: Great Doxology, Sunday Troparion "Today salvation is come to the world" (*Дне́сь спасе́ніе мі́рꙋ бы́сть*), the litanies, and the solemn Resurrectional dismissal.
[^5313]: SUNDAY DIVINE LITURGY (The Beatitudes, Tone 5). Heading for the Beatitude Troparia (*Блаже́нна*) appointed for the Sunday Divine Liturgy, Tone 5; Matthew 5:3-12.
[^5314]: SUNDAY DIVINE LITURGY (Beatitudes, Troparion 1). First Beatitude troparion, Tone 5; Luke 23:42.
[^5315]: SUNDAY DIVINE LITURGY (Beatitudes, Troparion 2). Second Beatitude troparion, Tone 5; Genesis 3:17; Galatians 3:13.
[^5316]: SUNDAY DIVINE LITURGY (Beatitudes, Troparion 3). Third Beatitude troparion, Tone 5; 1 Corinthians 15:20-22; Hebrews 2:14.
[^5317]: SUNDAY DIVINE LITURGY (Beatitudes, Troparion 4). Fourth Beatitude troparion, Tone 5; Mark 16:1-6.
[^5318]: SUNDAY DIVINE LITURGY (Beatitudes, Troparion 5). Fifth Beatitude troparion, Tone 5; Luke 23:39-43.
[^5319]: SUNDAY DIVINE LITURGY (Beatitudes, Troparion 6). Sixth Beatitude troparion, Tone 5; Psalm 18:5 [LXX]; Romans 10:18.
[^5320]: SUNDAY DIVINE LITURGY (Beatitudes, Triadikon). Trinitarian troparion (`Тро́йченъ`) at the Beatitudes, sung at *Glory...*; Isaiah 6:3.
[^5321]: SUNDAY DIVINE LITURGY (Beatitudes, Theotokion). Concluding Theotokion (`Бг҃оро́диченъ`) at the Beatitudes, sung at *Both now...*; Ezekiel 44:2; Isaiah 7:14; Matthew 1:23.
[^5322]: MONDAY LITURGICAL DAY HEADPIECE. Primary Day Headpiece for Monday, dedicated in the Byzantine weekly liturgical cycle to the Holy Bodiless Powers (Angels and Archangels).
[^5323]: SUNDAY EVENING (Vespers Opening). Rubric marking the beginning of Sunday evening services (*Въ Недѣ́лю ве́чера*), opening the liturgical day of Monday.
[^5324]: SUNDAY EVENING (Penitential Stichera Title). Genre rubric for the Penitential Stichera (*стїхи̑ры покаѧ́нны*) sung at "Lord, I have cried" (*На Гдⷭ҇и воззва́хъ*), Tone 5.
[^5325]: MELODIC MODEL HEADING. Canonical 3-layer melodic model specification: *Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice, O container of ascetics" (Ра́дꙋйсѧ по́стникѡвъ):*; Greek model: Χαίροις ἀσκητικῶν.
[^5326]: SUNDAY EVENING (Penitential Stichera, Sticheron 1, incipit). Opening portion of the first Penitential Sticheron on "Lord, I have cried," Tone 5, breaking off mid-word (*ѻ҆каѧ́н-*) at the bottom of physical leaf p530 (fol. 528v) and concluding (*-ный. Тѣ́мже молю́сѧ...*) on physical leaf p531 (fol. 529r); 2 Corinthians 5:10.
[^5327]: SUNDAY EVENING (Penitential Stichera, Sticheron 1, conclusion). Concluding section of the first Penitential Sticheron on "Lord, I have cried," Tone 5, completing the text begun on leaf p530; Mark 9:44, 48; Matthew 8:12.
[^5328]: SUNDAY EVENING (Penitential Stichera, Sticheron 2). Second Penitential Sticheron on "Lord, I have cried," Tone 5, modeled on *Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice, O container of ascetics" (Ра́дꙋйсѧ по́стникѡвъ):*; Psalm 118:136; Luke 15:18-19.
[^5329]: SUNDAY EVENING (Penitential Stichera, Sticheron 3). Third Penitential Sticheron on "Lord, I have cried," Tone 5; Romans 7:14-24.
[^5330]: SUNDAY EVENING (Stichera to the Bodiless Powers Title). Rubric introducing the three Stichera to the Bodiless Powers (*И҆́ны стїхи̑ры, Безпло́тнымъ*) on "Lord, I have cried," appointed for Sunday evening as the liturgical entry into Monday.
[^5331]: MELODIC MODEL REPETITION. Rubric indicating the same Tone and melodic model (*Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже*): *Tone 5, Podoben: "Rejoice, O container of ascetics" (Ра́дꙋйсѧ по́стникѡвъ):*; Greek model: Χαίροις ἀσκητικῶν.
[^5332]: SUNDAY EVENING (Stichera to the Bodiless Powers, Sticheron 1). First Sticheron to the Holy Bodiless Powers on "Lord, I have cried," Tone 5; Hebrews 1:7; Hebrews 4:15.
[^5333]: SUNDAY EVENING (Stichera to the Bodiless Powers, Sticheron 2). Second Sticheron to the Holy Bodiless Powers on "Lord, I have cried," Tone 5; Isaiah 6:1-3; John 1:5.
[^5334]: SUNDAY EVENING (Stichera to the Bodiless Powers, Sticheron 3, incipit). Opening portion of the third Sticheron to the Bodiless Powers on "Lord, I have cried," Tone 5, breaking off mid-word (*бѣ́д-*) at the foot of physical leaf p531 (fol. 529r) and concluding (*-ствꙋющихъ...*) on leaf p532 (fol. 530v); Hebrews 4:16.
[^5335]: SUNDAY EVENING (Stichera to the Bodiless Powers, Sticheron 3, conclusion). Conclusion of the third Sticheron to the Bodiless Powers on "Lord, I have cried," Tone 5, completing the text begun on leaf p531; Psalm 90:3.
[^5336]: SUNDAY EVENING (Lord I Have Cried, Theotokion). Dogmatic Theotokion (*Бг҃оро́диченъ*) sung at *Glory... Both now...* on "Lord, I have cried," Tone 5; Colossians 1:15-20.
[^5337]: SUNDAY EVENING (Ordinary Rubrics). Standard liturgical items following "Lord, I have cried": the evening hymn *Phos Hilaron* (*Свѣ́те ти́хїй*), the Great Prokeimenon of Sunday evening, and the evening prayer *Vouchsafe, O Lord* (*Сподо́би Гдⷭ҇и*).
[^5338]: SUNDAY EVENING (Aposticha Title). Liturgical heading for the Penitential Stichera at the Aposticha (*На Стїхо́внѣ, стїхи̑ры покаѧ́нны*), Tone 5.
[^5339]: SUNDAY EVENING (Aposticha, Sticheron 1). First Penitential Sticheron at the Aposticha, Tone 5.
[^5340]: SCRIPTURAL PSALM VERSE. Psalm 122:1-2 (LXX), the first prescribed verse for the Aposticha on weekday evenings.
[^5341]: SUNDAY EVENING (Aposticha, Sticheron 2). Second Penitential Sticheron at the Aposticha, Tone 5; Matthew 9:12.
[^5342]: SCRIPTURAL PSALM VERSE. Psalm 122:3-4 (LXX), the second prescribed verse for the Aposticha on weekday evenings.
[^5343]: SUNDAY EVENING (Aposticha, Martyrikon). Dismissal troparion/sticheron honoring the Holy Martyrs (*Мч҃ениченъ*) at the Aposticha, Tone 5; Hebrews 11:35-38.
[^5344]: SUNDAY EVENING (Aposticha, Theotokion, incipit). Opening portion of the concluding Theotokion at the Aposticha, Tone 5, breaking off mid-hymn at the foot of physical leaf p532 (fol. 530v) and concluding on leaf p533 (fol. 531r); Isaiah 7:14; Luke 1:34-35.
[^5345]: SUNDAY EVENING (Aposticha, Theotokion, conclusion). Concluding phrase of the Aposticha Theotokion, Tone 5, completing the text begun on leaf p532.
[^5346]: SUNDAY EVENING (Vespers Dismissal Rubrics). Prescribed closing order for Sunday Vespers: the Canticle of Simeon (*Ны́нѣ ѿпꙋща́еши*, Luke 2:29-32), Trisagion Prayers through the Lord's Prayer, Troparion of the Saint of the day from the Menaion, *Glory... Both now...* and matching Theotokion, concluding litany and liturgical dismissal.
[^5347]: SUNDAY COMPLINE HEADING. Service rubric designating Small Compline on Sunday evening (*Въ Недѣ́лю на ПОКЕЧЕ́РЇИ* — noting the printed archaic orthography with *к* for *в*).
